# AI Media Scout — 2026-11-09

Expanded open-source creative-AI discovery across 94 fields, 808 repository queries and 22 model tasks, with primary web research beyond GitHub. This edition completes earlier screened guides, adds newly screened creative workflows and records substantive JoyAI/HME/BeatDesign updates without calling them new launches. Source review only; no discovered application was installed or run. Weekly current-source review covers all 1,990 previously published entries; missing evidence remains explicit and prior reports are preserved. The pending appendix contains screened software/creative-AI relevance only, not full setup or quality recommendations. Raw, unreviewed, excluded and duplicate-only candidates stay local. Additional primary-site follow-up screened 17 source repositories: 6 had reviewed open-source grants and supported workflows, 6 had restricted current code and 5 had unresolved complete-grant evidence. Those outcomes are recorded separately from search-query coverage. Verified collection: 808/808 repository queries attempted; 808 successful, 0 partial, 0 failed and 126 bounded/truncated. All 22 model tasks were attempted; 0 failed. These are bounded discovery attempts, not an exhaustive census or quality approval. Separate from successful search queries, 39 watchlist fetches and 3 enrichment attempts failed; the detailed warnings remain in the report. A 404/409 is an availability gap, not proof of abandonment or license revocation.

Documentation reviewed today. Tools are not hands-on tested unless explicitly stated. Requirements and performance remain source-specific.

## Coverage

| Images & design | Invoke's current primary site was inspected for its image-creation workflow; today's image clients and ComfyUI integrations require their own full code grants and separate model/provider review. [Source 1](https://invoke.ai/) |

## Search and review counts

94 creative fields; 808/808 repository queries attempted; 22/22 model-task queries attempted. 31286 distinct source candidates and 919 model leads. 26 detailed profiles and 207 additional screened discoveries. Source gaps: 0 failed repository queries, 0 partial repository queries, 126 bounded repository queries, 0 failed model-task queries and 0 web ecosystem gaps. Raw search candidates include duplicates of known tools, excluded projects and projects awaiting review; they are not verified recommendations.

## Research beyond GitHub

- **gitlab** · searched: Current full-source attempts for AudioStellar, perso-ai, LibreTerrain, EmbeddedAmp, OpenDub and OpenSlides were reviewed in the weekly pass, with complete grants or explicit blocked code views. Live discovery also found PREMIERE AI Toolbox material, but no new full grant/implemented creative-tool screen was completed for it. [Source](https://gitlab.com/ayrsd/audiostellar/-/raw/audiostellar2/README.md) [Source](https://gitlab.com/ilsp-spmd-all/premiere_group/ai-toolbox/AIToolbox)
- **codeberg** · searched: Both existing project root overviews were readable. Current full-file/license requests returned HTTP200 bot challenges; they are recorded as unavailable evidence and no challenge was bypassed. No new eligible source was cleared. [Source](https://codeberg.org/Imaginer/Imaginer) [Source](https://codeberg.org/MightyOwlbear/Raspberry-Pi-5-Diffusion)
- **sourcehut** · searched: Current diffuser README, full MIT grant, Cargo manifest and inference source were inspected. Its author-local absolute dependency path prevents a portable unchanged checkout; no unrelated new project was cleared from this bounded source review. [Source](https://git.sr.ht/~rockerboo/diffuser/blob/main/Cargo.toml) [Source](https://git.sr.ht/~rockerboo/diffuser/blob/main/LICENSE)
- **packages** · searched: PyPI game-localizer/gameloc JSON and TensorStack NuGet metadata were inspected. Manimvox and Brandly HTML returned challenges rather than usable package documentation; package metadata alone never clears software/model rights or usability. [Source](https://pypi.org/pypi/game-localizer/json) [Source](https://pypi.org/pypi/gameloc/json) [Source](https://api.nuget.org/v3-flatcontainer/tensorstack.media/index.json)
- **creative-plugins** · searched: Interstice primary documentation and current Blender, ComfyUI, PhotoCraft, notation, subtitle, game and lighting integrations were inspected. A connector-only repository without implemented software was held; proprietary hosts and paid services remain separate. [Source](https://docs.interstice.cloud/) [Source](https://github.com/coreyx/photocraft-ai-diffusion)
- **project-sites** · searched: All 94observed fields received a live primary web-source attempt beyond GitHub:100 unique URLs,99 HTTP 200 responses and oneTLSfailure;3 PDF captures were bounded at2MB. Challenge/login pages, placeholders and thin JavaScript shells were not treated as usable source evidence or quality approval. [Source](https://tryonlabs.github.io/opentryon/) [Source](https://shopprentice.com/) [Source](https://lacylights.com/)
- **research-code** · searched: Primary papers/project pages covered motion, brain/music mappings, relighting, codecs, acoustic/haptic fields, tactile graphics, robotic calligraphy and other adjacent practices. Complete code grants are a separate admission gate; paper provenance and owner metrics do not grant quality labels. [Source](https://midotronn.github.io/MAESTRO/) [Source](https://google-research.github.io/seanet/brain2music/) [Source](https://luoprojectpage.github.io/callirewrite/)
- **international** · searched: Current primary multilingual projects include Japanese EEG/notation workflows, Chinese CAD/manga pipelines, Arabic game subtitles, Vietnamese translation, Portuguese accessibility, Korean game tooling and bilingual publication. Language/region never substitutes for license, creative AI use or quality evidence. [Source](https://abdallah28r.github.io/taarib/en/) [Source](https://github.com/BaselMGAG/glass_hud_translator) [Source](https://github.com/eliezer-tavares/clarifica-ai)

## Photo Organiser AI

AI photobook, album sequencing and editorial layout · Photography, restoration & color · Creative publishing & presentation

First complete guide in this library, including completion of any earlier screened entry; not a claim of a launch today.

### Quality assessment: Quality unverified

Software licensing and a concrete creative AI use are supported. The remaining checks do not establish a recommendation.

Scope: Organize a wedding or event shoot, then build an editable photo-story sequence and compose page layouts.

Evidence checked: 2026-11-09. No local execution implied.

- **Open-source software license · verified:** The complete root software license was read and archived. The grant covers this project code; unresolved model, service and asset terms are disclosed separately. [Source](https://github.com/Saquib764/photo-organiser-ai/blob/main/LICENSE)
- **Creative results · documented:** The README shows the process-management screen and documents the Photobook page preview. No independently evaluated print-proof or output-quality pass was established. [Source](https://github.com/Saquib764/photo-organiser-ai/blob/main/README.md)
- **Reproducible setup · documented:** Clone the official repository. Run the FastAPI backend with uv and build the Nuxt frontend with Yarn in a second terminal. Put photos in workspace/raw; save the provider key only in the local Settings tab before AI analysis. Independent reproduction has not been established in this review. [Source](https://github.com/Saquib764/photo-organiser-ai/blob/main/README.md)
- **Maintenance and support · unknown:** A release or active repository alone does not establish sustained support. Workflow-specific support and unresolved blockers need a deeper review.
- **Independent use · unknown:** Independent first-hand use or a working third-party integration was not established for this selected workflow.
- **Models, services and costs · documented:** Apache-2.0 covers this application code; OpenAI hosted-model access and terms remain separate. Local thumbnails do not need a model key; AI analysis does. Local storage/compute and paid OpenAI API usage; current prices and a per-album cost are not documented in the reviewed guide. [Source](https://github.com/Saquib764/photo-organiser-ai/blob/main/README.md)

Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. Images are local for library management, but AI analysis uses the configured external provider. Do not equate page previews with press-ready PDF/bleed/color validation; a verified print export and production print requirements were not established.

### How it uses AI

OpenAI vision creates captions and photo metadata, and an AI chat planner proposes an event photobook with page order, layouts, narratives and category selections. [Source](https://github.com/Saquib764/photo-organiser-ai/blob/main/README.md)

### Introduction

OpenAI vision creates captions and photo metadata, and an AI chat planner proposes an event photobook with page order, layouts, narratives and category selections. [Source 1](https://github.com/Saquib764/photo-organiser-ai/blob/main/README.md)

### What it is good for

Organize a wedding or event shoot, then build an editable photo-story sequence and compose page layouts. [Source 1](https://github.com/Saquib764/photo-organiser-ai/blob/main/README.md)

### Demo & examples

The README shows the process-management screen and documents the Photobook page preview. No independently evaluated print-proof or output-quality pass was established. [Source 1](https://github.com/Saquib764/photo-organiser-ai/blob/main/README.md)

### Install

Clone the official repository. Run the FastAPI backend with uv and build the Nuxt frontend with Yarn in a second terminal. Put photos in workspace/raw; save the provider key only in the local Settings tab before AI analysis. [Source 1](https://github.com/Saquib764/photo-organiser-ai/blob/main/README.md)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Clone the official repository. Run the FastAPI backend with uv and build the Nuxt frontend with Yarn in a second terminal. Put photos in workspace/raw; save the provider key only in the local Settings tab before AI analysis.

```sh
git clone https://github.com/Saquib764/photo-organiser-ai.git
```


```sh
cd photo-organiser-ai/backend
```


```sh
uv sync
```


```sh
uv run uvicorn app.main:app --host 127.0.0.1 --port 8001
```


```sh
cd ../frontend
```


```sh
yarn install
```


```sh
yarn build
```


```sh
yarn preview
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/Saquib764/photo-organiser-ai/blob/main/README.md)

1. Copy a small rights-cleared photo set into workspace/raw and start both services.
2. Run thumbnail resizing, palette analysis and AI analysis in order; inspect the captions and categories.
3. In Photobook, request an eight-page sequence, inspect page previews, use Compose and manually assign or replace images.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/Saquib764/photo-organiser-ai/blob/main/README.md)

- **Hardware:** Minimum system RAM, VRAM and total disk space are not documented in the reviewed instructions.
- **Software:** Python 3.12+, uv, Node.js and Yarn; Nuxt 4 frontend and FastAPI backend. An OpenAI API key is required for image analysis and photobook chat; the documented default model is gpt-4o-mini.
- **Platforms:** A local browser interface and server setup are documented. An OS compatibility matrix is not published; native Windows/macOS/Linux validation is not established.

### License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [Source 1](https://github.com/Saquib764/photo-organiser-ai/blob/main/LICENSE) [Source 2](https://github.com/Saquib764/photo-organiser-ai/blob/main/README.md)

- **Code:** Apache-2.0
- **Weights:** Apache-2.0 covers this application code; OpenAI hosted-model access and terms remain separate. Local thumbnails do not need a model key; AI analysis does.
- **Commercial:** Software use follows Apache-2.0. Apache-2.0 covers this application code; OpenAI hosted-model access and terms remain separate. Local thumbnails do not need a model key; AI analysis does.
- **Cost:** Local storage/compute and paid OpenAI API usage; current prices and a per-album cost are not documented in the reviewed guide.

### Why it merits attention

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. [Source 1](https://github.com/Saquib764/photo-organiser-ai/blob/main/README.md)

### Limitations

Images are local for library management, but AI analysis uses the configured external provider. Do not equate page previews with press-ready PDF/bleed/color validation; a verified print export and production print requirements were not established. [Source 1](https://github.com/Saquib764/photo-organiser-ai/blob/main/README.md)

## Local AI Sound Generator

AI seamless loops, sample design and DAW-ready stems · Audio, music & voice · Games & production pipelines

The repository was created on 2026-10-10. This is its first full guide in this library, not independent validation of a new release.

### Quality assessment: Quality unverified

Software licensing and a concrete creative AI use are supported. The remaining checks do not establish a recommendation.

Scope: Make game foley, ambience and a level-music draft, compare seeded alternatives, trim or loop a chosen take and approve it before copying into Unity.

Evidence checked: 2026-11-09. No local execution implied.

- **Open-source software license · verified:** The complete root software license was read and archived. The grant covers this project code; unresolved model, service and asset terms are disclosed separately. [Source](https://github.com/JatCool/local-ai-sound-generator/blob/main/LICENSE)
- **Creative results · documented:** The README supplies a complete door-creak request, approval and Unity integration example plus developer-measured timing on an RTX 3080. These are developer observations, not independent listening evidence. [Source](https://github.com/JatCool/local-ai-sound-generator/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/JatCool/local-ai-sound-generator/blob/main/docs/licenses.md) [Source](https://github.com/JatCool/local-ai-sound-generator/blob/main/docs/setup.md)
- **Reproducible setup · documented:** Clone beside the game project, point the local config at an existing ComfyUI install, review the model licenses, then install only the chosen model group. The documented launchers use ComfyUI portable Python or an explicitly configured interpreter. Independent reproduction has not been established in this review. [Source](https://github.com/JatCool/local-ai-sound-generator/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/JatCool/local-ai-sound-generator/blob/main/docs/licenses.md) [Source](https://github.com/JatCool/local-ai-sound-generator/blob/main/docs/setup.md)
- **Maintenance and support · unknown:** A release or active repository alone does not establish sustained support. Workflow-specific support and unresolved blockers need a deeper review.
- **Independent use · unknown:** Independent first-hand use or a working third-party integration was not established for this selected workflow.
- **Models, services and costs · documented:** MIT application code. The guide identifies ACE-Step 1.5 Turbo as MIT; Stable Audio 3 Small SFX instead uses the Stability AI Community License with commercial revenue and registration conditions. Model permissions must be checked separately. Local GPU/compute and model storage; Stable Audio commercial access has separate registration/revenue terms. No API subscription is required for the described local model routes. [Source](https://github.com/JatCool/local-ai-sound-generator/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/JatCool/local-ai-sound-generator/blob/main/docs/licenses.md) [Source](https://github.com/JatCool/local-ai-sound-generator/blob/main/docs/setup.md)

Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. Negative prompts barely affect CFG-1 SFX; very short clicks may need repeated candidates. Bar trimming assumes a first beat at zero; seam metrics are heuristics. Developer reproducibility and timing are version/hardware specific. The review service is local and single-user.

### How it uses AI

ComfyUI built-in audio-model workflows generate sound-effect candidates and music, with a local listen-and-approve review page and an explicit Unity asset handoff. [Source](https://github.com/JatCool/local-ai-sound-generator/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/JatCool/local-ai-sound-generator/blob/main/docs/licenses.md) [Source](https://github.com/JatCool/local-ai-sound-generator/blob/main/docs/setup.md)

### Introduction

ComfyUI built-in audio-model workflows generate sound-effect candidates and music, with a local listen-and-approve review page and an explicit Unity asset handoff. [Source 1](https://github.com/JatCool/local-ai-sound-generator/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/JatCool/local-ai-sound-generator/blob/main/docs/licenses.md) [Source 3](https://github.com/JatCool/local-ai-sound-generator/blob/main/docs/setup.md)

These are developer observations, not independent listening evidence. ### Install

Clone beside the game project, point the local config at an existing ComfyUI install, review the model licenses, then install only the chosen model group. The documented launchers use ComfyUI portable Python or an explicitly configured interpreter. [Source 1](https://github.com/JatCool/local-ai-sound-generator/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/JatCool/local-ai-sound-generator/blob/main/docs/licenses.md) [Source 3](https://github.com/JatCool/local-ai-sound-generator/blob/main/docs/setup.md)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Clone beside the game project, point the local config at an existing ComfyUI install, review the model licenses, then install only the chosen model group. The documented launchers use ComfyUI portable Python or an explicitly configured interpreter.

```sh
git clone https://github.com/JatCool/local-ai-sound-generator.git
```


```sh
cd local-ai-sound-generator
```


```sh
./sound-generator models list
```


```sh
./sound-generator doctor
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/JatCool/local-ai-sound-generator/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/JatCool/local-ai-sound-generator/blob/main/docs/licenses.md) [Source 3](https://github.com/JatCool/local-ai-sound-generator/blob/main/docs/setup.md)

1. Configure the local ComfyUI location and choose SFX or music only after reviewing the separate model terms.
2. Create one sound request, generate several candidates and open the local review page.
3. Listen to every candidate and inspect clipping, duration and loop seams before approving a take.
4. Use the explicit integration step to copy the approved WAV into the game; retain its prompt, seed and model record.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/JatCool/local-ai-sound-generator/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/JatCool/local-ai-sound-generator/blob/main/docs/licenses.md) [Source 3](https://github.com/JatCool/local-ai-sound-generator/blob/main/docs/setup.md)

- **Hardware:** NVIDIA CUDA GPU is documented; the developer tested an RTX 3080 10 GB, which is not a universal minimum. Model storage is 3.5 GB for SFX and 10 GB for music. A minimum system RAM figure is not documented.
- **Software:** Python 3.10+, NumPy and PyAV; ComfyUI 0.38.0 portable was tested by the developer. Only built-in nodes are used. Git Bash, cmd/PowerShell and a Python launcher are documented.
- **Platforms:** Windows 10/11 documented, Windows 11 developer-tested; Linux git ComfyUI route is untested. macOS path handling is implemented but untested and does not establish accelerator support.

### License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. - **Weights:** MIT application code. The guide identifies ACE-Step 1.5 Turbo as MIT; Stable Audio 3 Small SFX instead uses the Stability AI Community License with commercial revenue and registration conditions. Model permissions must be checked separately.
- **Commercial:** Software use follows MIT. MIT application code. The guide identifies ACE-Step 1.5 Turbo as MIT; Stable Audio 3 Small SFX instead uses the Stability AI Community License with commercial revenue and registration conditions. Model permissions must be checked separately.
- **Cost:** Local GPU/compute and model storage; Stable Audio commercial access has separate registration/revenue terms. No API subscription is required for the described local model routes.

### Why it merits attention

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. ## RC Stable Audio Tools

AI seamless loops, sample design and DAW-ready stems · Audio, music & voice

First complete guide in this library, including completion of any earlier screened entry; not a claim of a launch today.

### Quality assessment: Quality unverified

Software licensing and a concrete creative AI use are supported. The remaining checks do not establish a recommendation.

Scope: Explore DAW-ready loop ideas, generate one-shot samples, or export an instrument keybed to DecentSampler/SFZ when the selected model was trained for that mode.

Evidence checked: 2026-11-09. No local execution implied.

- **Open-source software license · verified:** The complete root software license was read and archived. The grant covers this project code; unresolved model, service and asset terms are disclosed separately. [Source](https://github.com/RoyalCities/RC-stable-audio-tools/blob/main/LICENSE)
- **Creative results · documented:** The README includes model-loader, timing, key-lock, trimming and MIDI demonstration images/GIFs. We did not listen to or benchmark generated audio. [Source](https://github.com/RoyalCities/RC-stable-audio-tools/blob/main/README.md)
- **Reproducible setup · documented:** Use a Python 3.10 virtual environment, install the repository package and launch run_gradio.py. Select/download a model in the first-run downloader, review its license and restart to enter the full interface. Independent reproduction has not been established in this review. [Source](https://github.com/RoyalCities/RC-stable-audio-tools/blob/main/README.md)
- **Maintenance and support · unknown:** A release or active repository alone does not establish sustained support. Workflow-specific support and unresolved blockers need a deeper review.
- **Independent use · unknown:** Independent first-hand use or a working third-party integration was not established for this selected workflow.
- **Models, services and costs · documented:** The complete MIT code grant is separate from every downloaded base model or finetune, MIDI/instrument assets and downstream sampler host. No blanket commercial model clearance is established. Local compute, downloaded checkpoints and any chosen sampler/DAW host; model and host costs/terms remain separate. [Source](https://github.com/RoyalCities/RC-stable-audio-tools/blob/main/README.md)

Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. Keybed and one-shot quality depend on a suitably trained model. MIDI conversion and exact trimming need human audition/editing. INT4 may be very slow on Windows; Linux TorchAO can require compatibility work. No universal minimum RAM/VRAM/disk is documented.

### How it uses AI

A generative-audio training/inference fork adds model-specific prompt builders, BPM/bar and key conditioning, automatic audio-to-MIDI conversion, and loop, one-shot and playable-keybed generation. [Source](https://github.com/RoyalCities/RC-stable-audio-tools/blob/main/README.md)

### Introduction

A generative-audio training/inference fork adds model-specific prompt builders, BPM/bar and key conditioning, automatic audio-to-MIDI conversion, and loop, one-shot and playable-keybed generation. [Source 1](https://github.com/RoyalCities/RC-stable-audio-tools/blob/main/README.md)

### What it is good for

Explore DAW-ready loop ideas, generate one-shot samples, or export an instrument keybed to DecentSampler/SFZ when the selected model was trained for that mode. [Source 1](https://github.com/RoyalCities/RC-stable-audio-tools/blob/main/README.md)

### Demo & examples

The README includes model-loader, timing, key-lock, trimming and MIDI demonstration images/GIFs. We did not listen to or benchmark generated audio. [Source 1](https://github.com/RoyalCities/RC-stable-audio-tools/blob/main/README.md)

### Install

Use a Python 3.10 virtual environment, install the repository package and launch run_gradio.py. Select/download a model in the first-run downloader, review its license and restart to enter the full interface. [Source 1](https://github.com/RoyalCities/RC-stable-audio-tools/blob/main/README.md)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Use a Python 3.10 virtual environment, install the repository package and launch run_gradio.py. Select/download a model in the first-run downloader, review its license and restart to enter the full interface.

```sh
git clone https://github.com/RoyalCities/RC-stable-audio-tools.git
```


```sh
cd RC-stable-audio-tools
```


```sh
python -m venv venv
```


```sh
pip install .
```

```sh
python run_gradio.py
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/RoyalCities/RC-stable-audio-tools/blob/main/README.md)

1. Activate the virtual environment using the documented command for your OS and configure output/model directories.
2. Download a chosen model with its configuration and license, then restart the UI.
3. Set BPM, bars and key or use a model-specific prompt builder; generate and audition a short sample.
4. Inspect the trim and MIDI conversion in your DAW; use keybed export only for a model trained for keybed support.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/RoyalCities/RC-stable-audio-tools/blob/main/README.md)

- **Hardware:** Minimum system RAM, VRAM and total disk space are not documented in the reviewed instructions.
- **Software:** Python 3.10 is the documented tested version; setup.py pins dependencies. Windows CUDA instructions specify PyTorch 2.5.1, torchvision 0.20.1 and torchaudio 2.5.1 with CUDA 12.1 wheels. Optional TorchAO support is version/platform dependent.
- **Platforms:** Windows, macOS and Linux setup commands are documented; the developer states Apple Silicon M1+ support. Optional INT4 instructions cover Windows/Linux and do not establish equivalent performance.

### License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [Source 1](https://github.com/RoyalCities/RC-stable-audio-tools/blob/main/LICENSE) [Source 2](https://github.com/RoyalCities/RC-stable-audio-tools/blob/main/README.md)

- **Code:** MIT
- **Weights:** The complete MIT code grant is separate from every downloaded base model or finetune, MIDI/instrument assets and downstream sampler host. No blanket commercial model clearance is established.
- **Commercial:** Software use follows MIT. The complete MIT code grant is separate from every downloaded base model or finetune, MIDI/instrument assets and downstream sampler host. No blanket commercial model clearance is established.
- **Cost:** Local compute, downloaded checkpoints and any chosen sampler/DAW host; model and host costs/terms remain separate.

### Why it merits attention

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. [Source 1](https://github.com/RoyalCities/RC-stable-audio-tools/blob/main/README.md)

### Limitations

Keybed and one-shot quality depend on a suitably trained model. MIDI conversion and exact trimming need human audition/editing. INT4 may be very slow on Windows; Linux TorchAO can require compatibility work. No universal minimum RAM/VRAM/disk is documented. [Source 1](https://github.com/RoyalCities/RC-stable-audio-tools/blob/main/README.md)

Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. Small parameter changes can cause large visual shifts. Legacy mode is frozen and retains old denoise/seed quirks. High-strength patterns can produce flashing/harsh artifacts. Sequence-latent painting is largely unexplored, and MiniMax H3 has batch-size-one constraints.

### How it uses AI

A ComfyUI custom sampler blends shader-generated structure into diffusion starting noise, letting artists explore variations around a fixed seed instead of changing it randomly. [Source](https://github.com/AEmotionStudio/ComfyUI-ShaderNoiseKSampler/blob/main/README.md)

### Introduction

A ComfyUI custom sampler blends shader-generated structure into diffusion starting noise, letting artists explore variations around a fixed seed instead of changing it randomly. [Source 1](https://github.com/AEmotionStudio/ComfyUI-ShaderNoiseKSampler/blob/main/README.md)

### What it is good for

Art-direct a diffusion composition with structured noise, compare neighboring variations, and explore controlled image/video noise patterns. [Source 1](https://github.com/AEmotionStudio/ComfyUI-ShaderNoiseKSampler/blob/main/README.md)

1. Add Shader Noise KSampler (Direct) in place of KSampler and connect model, conditioning and latent inputs.
2. Keep the seed fixed, choose the explore preset and start with low shader strength.
3. Inspect the live shader preview, queue a short test and compare nearby settings before expanding the experiment.
4. Use standard sampling for a new graph; inspect older workflows that load legacy mode.

- **Code:** GPL-3.0
- **Weights:** Full GPL-3.0 software grant reviewed. ComfyUI, checkpoints, VAEs and custom-node/model licenses are separate; a list of supported shapes is not universal model-rights clearance.
- **Commercial:** Software use follows GPL-3.0. Full GPL-3.0 software grant reviewed. ComfyUI, checkpoints, VAEs and custom-node/model licenses are separate; a list of supported shapes is not universal model-rights clearance.
- **Cost:** Local model compute and storage, plus any separately selected hosted node; no universal per-generation cost is documented.

### Why it merits attention

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. [Source 1](https://github.com/AEmotionStudio/ComfyUI-ShaderNoiseKSampler/blob/main/README.md)

### Limitations

Small parameter changes can cause large visual shifts. Legacy mode is frozen and retains old denoise/seed quirks. High-strength patterns can produce flashing/harsh artifacts. Sequence-latent painting is largely unexplored, and MiniMax H3 has batch-size-one constraints. [Source 1](https://github.com/AEmotionStudio/ComfyUI-ShaderNoiseKSampler/blob/main/README.md)

- [Repository](https://github.com/AEmotionStudio/ComfyUI-ShaderNoiseKSampler)
- [Documentation](https://github.com/AEmotionStudio/ComfyUI-ShaderNoiseKSampler/blob/main/README.md)

### Introduction

### What it is good for

Configure a real LLM/TTS provider before expecting spoken output. [Source 1](https://github.com/F31/ppts/blob/main/README.md)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. From web/, run npm ci and npm run build; return to the repository root for docker compose build and docker compose up -d. Configure actual LLM/TTS services.

```sh
git clone https://github.com/F31/ppts.git
```


```sh
npm ci
```


```sh
npm run build
```


```sh

```


```sh

```

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/F31/ppts/blob/main/README.md)

4. Enable the rendering/export dependencies, then review the MP4 and SRT/VTT timing against the original deck.

A linked full-speed video points to a different owner repository; that media link was not independently validated. No independent expressive-quality evaluation is established. Read the model/data restrictions before using or redistributing included joblib files. Independent reproduction has not been established in this review. [Source](https://github.com/raystermomo/NeuroCanvas/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/raystermomo/NeuroCanvas/blob/main/va_prediction_model/touchdesigner/README_TD.md)

The README says NeuroSense data and derivatives are subject to a restrictive Data Use Agreement while also shipping a trained model; rights to redistribute or commercially reuse that checkpoint are unresolved. TD-ML has its own MIT attribution. Muse hardware, phone/app and any required TouchDesigner license plus local compute. Current prices and commercial checkpoint clearance are not established. [Source](https://github.com/raystermomo/NeuroCanvas/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/raystermomo/NeuroCanvas/blob/main/va_prediction_model/touchdesigner/README_TD.md)

Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. The main README describes16-second windows and a2-second training step, while the detailed TD guide describes a4-second inference buffer and SVR/LightGBM averaging; these documents conflict. The detailed guide reports weak cross-subject CCC around0.17/0.08 and middle-biased output. Personal calibration may improve range but is not proof of emotion accuracy. No AI Media Scout execution or biological-signal validation was performed.

### How it uses AI

A LightGBM/SVR EEG mapping estimates valence/arousal controls and an MLP maps them to TouchDesigner visual parameters. EEG is an expressive input, not a validated emotion or mind-reading measurement. [Source](https://github.com/raystermomo/NeuroCanvas/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/raystermomo/NeuroCanvas/blob/main/va_prediction_model/touchdesigner/README_TD.md)

### Install

Clone this repository, create a Python 3.10+ environment and install requirements. Separately configure TouchDesigner to use the td-ml environment through TDPyEnvManager, excluding pyEDFlib from the runtime environment. Read the model/data restrictions before using or redistributing included joblib files. [Source 1](https://github.com/raystermomo/NeuroCanvas/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/raystermomo/NeuroCanvas/blob/main/va_prediction_model/touchdesigner/README_TD.md)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Clone this repository, create a Python 3.10+ environment and install requirements. Separately configure TouchDesigner to use the td-ml environment through TDPyEnvManager, excluding pyEDFlib from the runtime environment. Read the model/data restrictions before using or redistributing included joblib files.

```sh
git clone https://github.com/raystermomo/NeuroCanvas.git
```


```sh
cd NeuroCanvas
```


```sh
python -m venv .venv
```


```sh
pip install -r requirements.txt
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/raystermomo/NeuroCanvas/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/raystermomo/NeuroCanvas/blob/main/va_prediction_model/touchdesigner/README_TD.md)

1. Activate the environment with the documented command for your OS and configure the separate TouchDesigner environment.
2. Set Mind Monitor Raw EEG /muse/eeg streaming to the computer IP and port 5005 on the same WiFi.
3. Open EEG2Emotion_Visual.toe; enable OSC Time Slice and map four channels exactly TP9 AF7 AF8 TP10.
4. Point VA_Infer at the model and code directory, inspect incoming data, calibrate and connect valence/arousal to the visual synthesizer.
5. Treat movement as an artistic control signal; compare mappings and inspect the output before a live presentation.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/raystermomo/NeuroCanvas/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/raystermomo/NeuroCanvas/blob/main/va_prediction_model/touchdesigner/README_TD.md)

- **Hardware:** Muse 2 EEG headset, a Mind Monitor phone and a computer on the same WiFi are documented. Minimum RAM, GPU/VRAM, storage and measured end-to-end latency are not documented.
- **Software:** Python 3.10+, NumPy/SciPy, LightGBM, scikit-learn, joblib and the documented TD-ML environment; TouchDesigner and TDPyEnvManager. pyEDFlib is required for raw-data training, not the dependency-light runtime feature extractor.
- **Platforms:** The guide provides shell and Windows activation examples but no tested operating-system/TouchDesigner-version matrix. A browser or Python script does not establish Linux support for the complete TouchDesigner workflow.

The README says NeuroSense data and derivatives are subject to a restrictive Data Use Agreement while also shipping a trained model; rights to redistribute or commercially reuse that checkpoint are unresolved. TD-ML has its own MIT attribution.
- **Commercial:** Software use follows MIT. Complete MIT software grant reviewed. TouchDesigner, Mind Monitor and Muse hardware are separate. ## ComposerLint

AI music notation and score recovery · Interactive, immersive & live media

The repository was created on 2026-11-07. This is its first full guide in this library, not independent validation of a new release.

### Quality assessment: Quality unverified

Software licensing and a concrete creative AI use are supported. The remaining checks do not establish a recommendation.

Scope: Discuss harmony, motifs or ensemble relationships; inspect a short pitch/register or expressive alternative while keeping the original score intact.

Evidence checked: 2026-11-09. No local execution implied.

- **Open-source software license · verified:** The complete root software license was read and archived. The grant covers this project code; unresolved model, service and asset terms are disclosed separately. Included patch GPLv3 body matches the complete reviewed standard, and the complete LGPLv3 additional permissions were read, including combined-work source/relinking and notice conditions. This is source-only clearance; binary/font redistribution remains unresolved. [Source](https://github.com/kyc-212/ComposerLint/blob/main/LICENSE) [Source](https://github.com/kyc-212/ComposerLint/blob/main/LICENSES/third-party/verovio/COPYING) [Source](https://github.com/kyc-212/ComposerLint/blob/main/LICENSES/third-party/verovio/COPYING.LESSER)
- **Creative results · documented:** Authored examples and viewer/editing workflows are documented. The developer calls candidate checks independent within the software; this is not evidence of an independent human user or artistic improvement. [Source](https://github.com/kyc-212/ComposerLint/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/kyc-212/ComposerLint/blob/main/THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md)
- **Reproducible setup · documented:** The demonstrated source installation uses CPython 3.12, uv and Apple Command Line Tools on Apple Silicon. The source install.sh offers text-only review or a full local patched-renderer build after its plan is reviewed. A graphical ZIP is conditional on an actual release asset, unsigned and not guaranteed published. Independent reproduction has not been established in this review. [Source](https://github.com/kyc-212/ComposerLint/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/kyc-212/ComposerLint/blob/main/THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md)
- **Maintenance and support · unknown:** A release or active repository alone does not establish sustained support. Workflow-specific support and unresolved blockers need a deeper review.
- **Independent use · unknown:** Independent first-hand use or a working third-party integration was not established for this selected workflow.
- **Models, services and costs · documented:** Complete MIT grant covers ComposerLint-owned code. The included Verovio patch is LGPL-3.0-only with separate GPL/LGPL texts and attribution. Renderer binaries, fonts and downloaded environments are excluded; the notices expressly leave binary/font redistribution clearance incomplete. No score/repertoire rights are granted. Local analysis/renderer compute plus the selected AI client/model account and optional external notation host; no universal service-free or free-commercial redistribution claim is made. [Source](https://github.com/kyc-212/ComposerLint/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/kyc-212/ComposerLint/blob/main/THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md)

Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. MIDI is analysis-only. General-purpose notation editing, playback, PDF/audio input and arbitrary restructuring are unavailable. Exact pitch previews cover1–8 contiguous measures/onepart; expressive/new-note paths have narrower1–4 measure policies. M5 previews cannot use the M6 export route. Structural checks do not establish musical quality. No independent creator reproduction or installation was performed.

### How it uses AI

Local MCP analysis and agent skills interpret score evidence and propose bounded MusicXML edits, with separate structural checks and a human-operated export decision. ## Ultrastar Creator Tool

Audio, music & voice · AI music notation and score recovery · Games & production pipelines

First complete guide in this library, including completion of any earlier screened entry; not a claim of a launch today.

### Quality assessment: Quality unverified

Software licensing and a concrete creative AI use are supported. The remaining checks do not establish a recommendation.

Scope: Create a personal karaoke arrangement from authorized audio, repair syllable timing and pitches by ear, and export an UltraStar song folder or TXT.

Evidence checked: 2026-11-09. No local execution implied.

- **Open-source software license · verified:** The complete root software license was read and archived. The grant covers this project code; unresolved model, service and asset terms are disclosed separately. [Source](https://github.com/retotito/UltrastarCreatorTool/blob/main/LICENSE)
- **Creative results · documented:** The README shows the piano roll and links tutorials for earlier versions. Its six-song syllable-error improvement is an owner measurement, not an independent test or a universal accuracy claim. [Source](https://github.com/retotito/UltrastarCreatorTool/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/retotito/UltrastarCreatorTool/blob/main/docs/architecture/ai-models.md)
- **Reproducible setup · documented:** Choose a matching official macOS DMG or Windows MSI release; first launch downloads about 3.5 GB of models. The app is unsigned. Alternatively use Python 3.12, Node 18+ and FFmpeg to run the backend and frontend separately; IntelMac needs the older packages specified in its build workflow. Independent reproduction has not been established in this review. [Source](https://github.com/retotito/UltrastarCreatorTool/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/retotito/UltrastarCreatorTool/blob/main/docs/architecture/ai-models.md)
- **Maintenance and support · unknown:** A release or active repository alone does not establish sustained support. Workflow-specific support and unresolved blockers need a deeper review.
- **Independent use · unknown:** Independent first-hand use or a working third-party integration was not established for this selected workflow.
- **Models, services and costs · documented:** Complete GPL-3.0 software grant reviewed. BPM detection uses separately AGPL-3.0 Essentia. The model document identifies MIT FCPE, CC-BY-NC 4.0 singing aligner and a noncommercial GAME note model; other separation/Whisper dependencies keep their own terms. The software grant does not clear commercial model/output use or song rights. Local compute/storage and authorized music assets; separate noncommercial model restrictions can block commercial workflows despite free software. [Source](https://github.com/retotito/UltrastarCreatorTool/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/retotito/UltrastarCreatorTool/blob/main/docs/architecture/ai-models.md)

Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. Automatic lyric and pitch estimates need listening and correction. Tutorials show older UI. Some models restrict commercial use, and fallback manual modes do not validate the neural routes. Before opening an unsigned installer, review its source and actual release; no security controls were changed by this research.

### How it uses AI

Neural vocal separation, lyric alignment and singing-pitch/note detection produce an editable UltraStar karaoke score, with a piano-roll correction workflow. [Source](https://github.com/retotito/UltrastarCreatorTool/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/retotito/UltrastarCreatorTool/blob/main/docs/architecture/ai-models.md)

### Introduction

Neural vocal separation, lyric alignment and singing-pitch/note detection produce an editable UltraStar karaoke score, with a piano-roll correction workflow. [Source 1](https://github.com/retotito/UltrastarCreatorTool/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/retotito/UltrastarCreatorTool/blob/main/docs/architecture/ai-models.md)

### What it is good for

Create a personal karaoke arrangement from authorized audio, repair syllable timing and pitches by ear, and export an UltraStar song folder or TXT. [Source 1](https://github.com/retotito/UltrastarCreatorTool/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/retotito/UltrastarCreatorTool/blob/main/docs/architecture/ai-models.md)

### Install

Choose a matching official macOS DMG or Windows MSI release; first launch downloads about 3.5 GB of models. The app is unsigned. [Source 1](https://github.com/retotito/UltrastarCreatorTool/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/retotito/UltrastarCreatorTool/blob/main/docs/architecture/ai-models.md)

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/retotito/UltrastarCreatorTool/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/retotito/UltrastarCreatorTool/blob/main/docs/architecture/ai-models.md)

1. Activate the Python environment; source commands start the backend on 8001 and the frontend in a second terminal on 5173.
2. Import rights-cleared audio, supply or correct lyrics and select the permitted separation/alignment routes.
3. Inspect every note and syllable in the piano roll using loop/scrub and manual corrections; inspect duet singers separately.
4. Export a new song folder or TXT. Users upgrading from before5.0 are told to export a ZIP before removing the older app.

## MangaFlow

AI comic localization, relettering and translation · Images & design · Creative publishing & presentation

First complete guide in this library, including completion of any earlier screened entry; not a claim of a launch today.

### Quality assessment: Quality unverified

Software licensing and a concrete creative AI use are supported. The remaining checks do not establish a recommendation.

Scope: Translate authorized manga pages, correct masks and dialogue placement, and retain a project that can be revised after export.

Evidence checked: 2026-11-09. No local execution implied.

- **Open-source software license · verified:** The complete root software license was read and archived. The grant covers this project code; unresolved model, service and asset terms are disclosed separately. [Source](https://github.com/ALittlePandaMan/MangaFlow/blob/main/LICENSE)
- **Creative results · documented:** The owner documents anonymous container-pull/deployment checks and structured PSD/composite checks. Actual Photoshop editing compatibility and client-specific AI visual review still require testing; no independent translation/art-quality pass is established. [Source](https://github.com/ALittlePandaMan/MangaFlow/blob/main/README.md)
- **Reproducible setup · documented:** Choose a published0.1.4 deployment ZIP and Linux amd64 Docker Compose 2.30+. Default GPU mode requires compatible NVIDIA/CUDA 12.6container access; Windows uses WSL2/DockerDesktop Bash. CPU 0.1.4 images are separate and slower. Run the included pull/up scripts, then explicitly install local models and configure a translation provider. Independent reproduction has not been established in this review. [Source](https://github.com/ALittlePandaMan/MangaFlow/blob/main/README.md)
- **Maintenance and support · unknown:** A release or active repository alone does not establish sustained support. Workflow-specific support and unresolved blockers need a deeper review.
- **Independent use · unknown:** Independent first-hand use or a working third-party integration was not established for this selected workflow.
- **Models, services and costs · documented:** Complete MIT software grant reviewed. Weights/fonts/data remain separate; the KoharuRF-DETR model uses Manga109-derived training data whose terms require review. MIT code does not clear model use, comic page rights, uploaded fonts or external provider services. Local storage/GPU and downloads plus any separately chosen translation/OCR service charges. No universal commercial model/output clearance is established. [Source](https://github.com/ALittlePandaMan/MangaFlow/blob/main/README.md)

Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. The browser has no user login/public-access isolation and defaults to localhost. CloudOCR/translation sends selected images/text to providers. PSD special typography may be rasterized with hidden text backup. Encrypted/split archives are unsupported. Protocol tests do not prove an AI client can visually review pages. Minimum RAM/VRAM/storage are not published in the reviewed overview.

### How it uses AI

A local-first canvas combines learned layout detection, OCR, language-model translation, LaMa cleanup and editable typesetting for comic localization. [Source](https://github.com/ALittlePandaMan/MangaFlow/blob/main/README.md)

### Introduction

A local-first canvas combines learned layout detection, OCR, language-model translation, LaMa cleanup and editable typesetting for comic localization. [Source 1](https://github.com/ALittlePandaMan/MangaFlow/blob/main/README.md)

### What it is good for

Translate authorized manga pages, correct masks and dialogue placement, and retain a project that can be revised after export. [Source 1](https://github.com/ALittlePandaMan/MangaFlow/blob/main/README.md)

### Demo & examples

The owner documents anonymous container-pull/deployment checks and structured PSD/composite checks. Actual Photoshop editing compatibility and client-specific AI visual review still require testing; no independent translation/art-quality pass is established. [Source 1](https://github.com/ALittlePandaMan/MangaFlow/blob/main/README.md)

### Install

Choose a published0.1.4 deployment ZIP and Linux amd64 Docker Compose 2.30+. Default GPU mode requires compatible NVIDIA/CUDA 12.6container access; Windows uses WSL2/DockerDesktop Bash. CPU 0.1.4 images are separate and slower. Run the included pull/up scripts, then explicitly install local models and configure a translation provider. [Source 1](https://github.com/ALittlePandaMan/MangaFlow/blob/main/README.md)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Choose a published0.1.4 deployment ZIP and Linux amd64 Docker Compose 2.30+. Default GPU mode requires compatible NVIDIA/CUDA 12.6container access; Windows uses WSL2/DockerDesktop Bash. CPU 0.1.4 images are separate and slower. Run the included pull/up scripts, then explicitly install local models and configure a translation provider.

```sh
cd mangaflow-docker-0.1.4
```


```sh
bash scripts/docker.sh pull
```


```sh
bash scripts/docker.sh up -d
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/ALittlePandaMan/MangaFlow/blob/main/README.md)

1. Back up existing configuration, database and images before upgrading; retain private.env/config and volumes.
2. Open localhost:8080 and install the recommended detection/OCR/inpainting components only after reviewing upstream model terms.
3. Configure a real translation provider; example endpoints and passthrough do not translate.
4. Import rights-cleared pages, check detected text and reading order, then translate, repair masks/backgrounds and adjust typography.
5. Compare source/output pages for mistranslation, erased artwork and overflow; export images, CBZ, PSD or a resumable projectZIP.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/ALittlePandaMan/MangaFlow/blob/main/README.md)

- **Hardware:** Minimum system RAM, VRAM and total disk space are not documented in the reviewed instructions.
- **Software:** Docker Compose 2.30+, Linux amd64 images; GPU mode CUDA12.6/NVIDIA Container Toolkit as applicable. Local connector Node 20.11+. Source development uses Python 3.11/3.12, Node 20.11+ and PostgreSQL. Recommended local models include KoharuRF-DETR, PaddleOCR-VL1.6 and LaMa.
- **Platforms:** Linux amd64 containers and Windows WSL2/Docker Desktop are documented. A native macOS/Apple Silicon deployment is not established by this Docker/browser interface. CPU images exist only from0.1.4 and are discouraged for batch work without accepting slow processing.

### License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [Source 1](https://github.com/ALittlePandaMan/MangaFlow/blob/main/LICENSE) [Source 2](https://github.com/ALittlePandaMan/MangaFlow/blob/main/README.md)

- **Code:** MIT
- **Weights:** Complete MIT software grant reviewed. Weights/fonts/data remain separate; the KoharuRF-DETR model uses Manga109-derived training data whose terms require review. MIT code does not clear model use, comic page rights, uploaded fonts or external provider services.
- **Commercial:** Software use follows MIT. Complete MIT software grant reviewed. Weights/fonts/data remain separate; the KoharuRF-DETR model uses Manga109-derived training data whose terms require review. MIT code does not clear model use, comic page rights, uploaded fonts or external provider services.
- **Cost:** Local storage/GPU and downloads plus any separately chosen translation/OCR service charges. No universal commercial model/output clearance is established.

### Why it merits attention

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. [Source 1](https://github.com/ALittlePandaMan/MangaFlow/blob/main/README.md)

### Limitations

The browser has no user login/public-access isolation and defaults to localhost. CloudOCR/translation sends selected images/text to providers. PSD special typography may be rasterized with hidden text backup. Encrypted/split archives are unsupported. Protocol tests do not prove an AI client can visually review pages. Minimum RAM/VRAM/storage are not published in the reviewed overview. [Source 1](https://github.com/ALittlePandaMan/MangaFlow/blob/main/README.md)

### Quality assessment: Quality unverified

- **Open-source software license · verified:** The complete root software license was read and archived. The grant covers this project code; unresolved model, service and asset terms are disclosed separately. [Source](https://github.com/DrunkTrader/rigged_audio/blob/main/LICENSE)

- **Reproducible setup · documented:** Choose the official Windows/macOS/Linux package and inspect its release checksums. First launch downloads a private Python/PyTorch/Demucs/FFmpeg/yt-dlp engine. Alternatively clone and use Node 22+ with npm install and npm run dev. Unsigned installers require individual source/release review; this research did not change OS security settings. Independent reproduction has not been established in this review. [Source](https://github.com/DrunkTrader/rigged_audio/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/DrunkTrader/rigged_audio/blob/main/docs/QUICK_START.md)
- **Maintenance and support · unknown:** A release or active repository alone does not establish sustained support. Workflow-specific support and unresolved blockers need a deeper review.
- **Independent use · unknown:** Independent first-hand use or a working third-party integration was not established for this selected workflow.
- **Models, services and costs · documented:** Complete GPL-3.0 application grant reviewed. Demucs, PyTorch, uv, FFmpeg, yt-dlp and actual downloaded checkpoints retain their own terms. The app license does not clear the source music or platform download terms. Local compute/storage and source-media rights; no application account/subscription is documented for the local workflow. [Source](https://github.com/DrunkTrader/rigged_audio/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/DrunkTrader/rigged_audio/blob/main/docs/QUICK_START.md)

Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. Stems can contain bleed/artifacts and require listening. Owner CPU timing around twice song duration is not a guaranteed performance figure. YouTube features require internet and can fail or be restricted; local separation remains separate. Updates/uninstall preserve the user media/engine folder.

### How it uses AI

A local Demucs interface separates music into six stems and provides waveform mixing and WAV export. [Source](https://github.com/DrunkTrader/rigged_audio/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/DrunkTrader/rigged_audio/blob/main/docs/QUICK_START.md)

### Introduction

A local Demucs interface separates music into six stems and provides waveform mixing and WAV export. [Source 1](https://github.com/DrunkTrader/rigged_audio/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/DrunkTrader/rigged_audio/blob/main/docs/QUICK_START.md)

### What it is good for

Make a practice mix, isolate a vocal or rhythm section, or prepare stems from audio you are authorized to process. [Source 1](https://github.com/DrunkTrader/rigged_audio/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/DrunkTrader/rigged_audio/blob/main/docs/QUICK_START.md)

### Install

Choose the official Windows/macOS/Linux package and inspect its release checksums. First launch downloads a private Python/PyTorch/Demucs/FFmpeg/yt-dlp engine. Alternatively clone and use Node 22+ with npm install and npm run dev. Unsigned installers require individual source/release review; this research did not change OS security settings. [Source 1](https://github.com/DrunkTrader/rigged_audio/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/DrunkTrader/rigged_audio/blob/main/docs/QUICK_START.md)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Choose the official Windows/macOS/Linux package and inspect its release checksums. First launch downloads a private Python/PyTorch/Demucs/FFmpeg/yt-dlp engine. Alternatively clone and use Node 22+ with npm install and npm run dev. Unsigned installers require individual source/release review; this research did not change OS security settings.

```sh
git clone https://github.com/DrunkTrader/rigged_audio.git
```


```sh
cd rigged_audio
```


```sh
npm install
```


```sh
npm run dev
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/DrunkTrader/rigged_audio/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/DrunkTrader/rigged_audio/blob/main/docs/QUICK_START.md)

1. Complete the one-time engine download with sufficient free disk space.
2. Drop in authorized audio of up to 20 minutes and request six-stem separation.
3. Audition every stem, adjust mute/solo/volume or a preset, and check artifacts or bleed before export.
4. Export a stem, all stems or the heard mix as 16/24-bit or32-bit float WAV at original rate,44.1 kHz or 48 kHz.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/DrunkTrader/rigged_audio/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/DrunkTrader/rigged_audio/blob/main/docs/QUICK_START.md)

- **Hardware:** Documented 8 GB RAM or more and 8 GB free setup disk. Playback retains all stems in memory, about 2.5 GB for 20 minutes. GPU is optional; minimum VRAM and GPU-model-specific performance are not documented.
- **Software:** Packaged private runtime downloads about1 GB on CPU or3 GB with NVIDIA; source requires Node 22+. Demucs/PyTorch, FFmpeg and yt-dlp are installed under their own terms. AppImages require FUSE2 on documented Linux distributions.
- **Platforms:** Windows 10/11x64, macOS 12+Intel/Apple Silicon and64-bit Linux glibc 2.35+(Ubuntu22.04+) are documented. Optional NVIDIA acceleration is Windows/Linux; AMDROCm is Linux; Apple Silicon acceleration is described. CPU remains available.

### How it uses AI

JoyAI-Video-Edit 1.1 releases a new DiT checkpoint for reference-image-guided character replacement while preserving the source motion in a causal, streaming video editor. 3. Use the hardware-specific launcher from DEPLOYMENT.md; the 5090 route disables local prompt enhancement. Set JOYOMNI_HOST=127.0.0.1 when local-only access is intended.
4. Load a short clip or camera and authorized reference, request a character change, then inspect identity, source motion, temporal drift and export.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/jd-opensource/JoyAI-Video-Edit/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/jd-opensource/JoyAI-Video-Edit/blob/main/DEPLOYMENT.md) [Source 3](https://huggingface.co/jdopensource/JoyAI-Video-Edit) [Source 4](https://huggingface.co/spaces/wxDai/joyai-video-edit)

- **Hardware:** Owner-tested RTX 5090 32 GB at 840×480/24 FPS; video-only low-VRAM layout measured about 21.5 GiB resident and 28 GiB peak under a 30 GiB allocator cap. Full 720p/16 FPS layout needs about 46.5 GB steady. B200 reaches the separate reported 720p/30 FPS configuration. RAM and total required storage are not documented; optional Qwen weights alone add about 20.4 GiB.
- **Software:** Linux x86_64, Python 3.10, PyTorch 2.9.1 CUDA 12.8 wheels and nvcc 12.8+ for Blackwell. Compiled joyomni_ops, Triton and GPU-specific attention configuration are required. FA4 B200 and patched SageAttention RTX 5090 routes differ.
- **Platforms:** Linux/NVIDIA deployment is documented. Native macOS and Windows operation is not established by the browser client.

Householder Motion Embedding conditions CogVideoX image-to-video on a reference optical-flow field by modifying the initial noise. ### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/coldsilence507/HouseholderMotionEmbedding-Release/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://huggingface.co/anujeremylin/HouseholderMotionEmbedding) [Source 3](https://huggingface.co/THUDM/CogVideoX-5b-I2V/blob/main/LICENSE) [Source 4](https://coldsilence507.github.io/HouseholderMotionEmbedding/)

1. Review the CogVideoX base-model commercial registration and use conditions separately from this Apache-2.0 code.
2. Download the released LoRA explicitly and pass its real location using --lora_path in the documented inference command.
3. Supply one image plus a short reference video or the documented 48×2×240×360 flow tensor.
4. Check that the LoRA actually loaded: a missing file warns and falls back to the base model, which is not the proposed method.
5. Inspect the 49-frame 480×720, 8 FPS result; background motion can remain because inference does not apply the benchmark object mask.

Current screenshots render the real shared Compose UI using demo data. Android images are Roborazzi captures and desktop images are ImageComposeScene output; neither proves physical-device audio acceptance. [Source 1](https://github.com/bloodf/velum/blob/main/README.md)

### Install

Build a development Android APK using Git, JDK 17, Android SDK Platform 36 and the included Gradle wrapper. Select a supported local model or OpenAI-compatible API in Settings, then try a small recording. A store-ready release is not announced. [Source 1](https://github.com/bloodf/velum/blob/main/README.md)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Build a development Android APK using Git, JDK 17, Android SDK Platform 36 and the included Gradle wrapper. Select a supported local model or OpenAI-compatible API in Settings, then try a small recording. A store-ready release is not announced.

```sh
git clone https://github.com/bloodf/velum.git
```


```sh
cd velum
```


```sh
./gradlew :composeApp:assembleDebug
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/bloodf/velum/blob/main/README.md)

1. Install the reviewed development build on a compatible Android device; the output APK path is documented in the README.
2. Choose and download a supported local model, or configure the intended API endpoint and its model list.
3. Record or import a short rights-cleared interview, create a transcript and inspect timestamps and speaker labels.
4. Apply an interview template, compare notes against the audio and export; keep optional integrations off unless explicitly needed.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/bloodf/velum/blob/main/README.md)

- **Hardware:** A compatible Android device for the primary route; minimum RAM, GPU/VRAM and total storage are not documented. Optional recorder/device support remains unverified.
- **Software:** Git, JDK 17 and Android SDK Platform 36; Android targetAPI 35 with minimumAPI 26. Kotlin/Compose, SQLDelight and configurable local/API engines are documented. Exact model memory/storage requirements depend on the selected model and are not quantified in the overview.
- **Platforms:** Android is the current development/acceptance target. macOS microphone/system-audio and packaging acceptance are unfinished; Windows/Linux are outside this phase and iOS is deferred. Shared source targets do not establish working support.

### License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [Source 1](https://github.com/bloodf/velum/blob/main/LICENSE) [Source 2](https://github.com/bloodf/velum/blob/main/README.md)

- **Code:** Apache-2.0
- **Weights:** Complete Apache-2.0 software grant reviewed. Individual local models, external APIs and hardware protocols keep separate terms. The app does not require a Velum account/server/subscription, while external providers may charge and receive request data.
- **Commercial:** Software use follows Apache-2.0. Complete Apache-2.0 software grant reviewed. Individual local models, external APIs and hardware protocols keep separate terms. The app does not require a Velum account/server/subscription, while external providers may charge and receive request data.
- **Cost:** Local device/storage or the chosen external provider charges. Plaud hardware is optional and separate.

### Why it merits attention

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. [Source 1](https://github.com/bloodf/velum/blob/main/README.md)

### Limitations

Full Android regression/device acceptance remains open. PlaudNotePro BLE/WiFi integration is not real-hardware validated and is optional; a Plaud recorder is unnecessary for phone capture/import. No independent transcript/summary quality or hardware test was performed. [Source 1](https://github.com/bloodf/velum/blob/main/README.md)

### Get the tool

- [Repository](https://github.com/bloodf/velum)
- [Documentation](https://github.com/bloodf/velum/blob/main/README.md)

### Introduction

3. Import a draft and approved facts; review each flagged claim and its source rather than trusting the dashboard score.
4. If using the optional AI helper, review what text leaves the server and compare every rewrite; a person accepts findings and approves publication.

- **Platforms:** A local browser/server route is documented; an OS-specific tested compatibility matrix is not. Docker and CI files were authored but not run in the developer build environment.

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/edwardaiwang-svg/kinodraw/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/edwardaiwang-svg/kinodraw/blob/main/THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md) [Source 3](https://github.com/edwardaiwang-svg/kinodraw/blob/main/LICENSES/ASSETS.md)

1. Write a short plain-text, Markdown or DOCX script; use title/section headings for the agenda.
2. Start with local voice and offline planning, or explicitly configure an AI director or voice server.
3. Inspect the storyboard, replace ambiguous doodles and correct labels before rendering.
4. Watch the complete MP4 and listen to its narration/captions; retain script, chapters and transcript for revision.

## Intel Open Image Denoise

Neural denoising for real-time creative rendering · 3D, reconstruction & assets · VFX, compositing & relighting

First complete guide in this library, including completion of any earlier screened entry; not a claim of a launch today.

### Quality assessment: Quality unverified

Software licensing and a concrete creative AI use are supported. The remaining checks do not establish a recommendation.

Scope: Integrate neural denoising into a renderer or compare denoised preview/final frames against reference renders. This is a rendering SDK, not a general photo editor.

Evidence checked: 2026-11-09. No local execution implied.

- **Open-source software license · verified:** The complete root software license was read and archived. The grant covers this project code; unresolved model, service and asset terms are disclosed separately. [Source](https://github.com/RenderKit/oidn/blob/master/LICENSE.txt)
- **Creative results · documented:** The official site publishes denoising examples and the repository includes oidnDenoise/oidnBenchmark. No independent scene/temporal-quality assessment was completed in this first profile. [Source](https://github.com/RenderKit/oidn/blob/master/README.md)
- **Reproducible setup · documented:** Choose the matching official release or build the recursive Git LFS checkout. A basic source build requires CMake 3.15+, a C++11 compiler and Python 3; CPU builds also require ISPC 1.30+ and TBB 2017+. GPU backends have separate compiler/driver prerequisites in the README. Independent reproduction has not been established in this review. [Source](https://github.com/RenderKit/oidn/blob/master/README.md)
- **Maintenance and support · unknown:** A release or active repository alone does not establish sustained support. Workflow-specific support and unresolved blockers need a deeper review.
- **Independent use · unknown:** Independent first-hand use or a working third-party integration was not established for this selected workflow.
- **Models, services and costs · documented:** Complete Apache-2.0 software grant, including patent/NOTICE/redistribution conditions, was compared in full. Optional drivers, rendering hosts, third-party components and user-trained model/data rights remain separate. This review does not clear redistribution of every custom checkpoint. Local compute and any chosen renderer/driver stack. Apache code does not require an OIDN subscription; host licensing is separate. [Source](https://github.com/RenderKit/oidn/blob/master/README.md)

Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. Denoising can lose detail or introduce artifacts; a fast preview is not ground truth. Auxiliary-buffer conventions, device memory, GPU buffer APIs and image-format support matter. A renderer must use its actual integrated version. Universal RAM/VRAM/disk minima were not established in this review.

### How it uses AI

Deep-learning filters remove Monte Carlo noise from ray-traced renders, using beauty color and optionally albedo/normal buffers through a C/C++ API or example CLI. [Source](https://github.com/RenderKit/oidn/blob/master/README.md)

### Introduction

Deep-learning filters remove Monte Carlo noise from ray-traced renders, using beauty color and optionally albedo/normal buffers through a C/C++ API or example CLI. [Source 1](https://github.com/RenderKit/oidn/blob/master/README.md)

### What it is good for

Integrate neural denoising into a renderer or compare denoised preview/final frames against reference renders. This is a rendering SDK, not a general photo editor. [Source 1](https://github.com/RenderKit/oidn/blob/master/README.md)

### Demo & examples

The official site publishes denoising examples and the repository includes oidnDenoise/oidnBenchmark. No independent scene/temporal-quality assessment was completed in this first profile. [Source 1](https://github.com/RenderKit/oidn/blob/master/README.md)

### Install

Choose the matching official release or build the recursive Git LFS checkout. A basic source build requires CMake 3.15+, a C++11 compiler and Python 3; CPU builds also require ISPC 1.30+ and TBB 2017+. GPU backends have separate compiler/driver prerequisites in the README. [Source 1](https://github.com/RenderKit/oidn/blob/master/README.md)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Choose the matching official release or build the recursive Git LFS checkout. A basic source build requires CMake 3.15+, a C++11 compiler and Python 3; CPU builds also require ISPC 1.30+ and TBB 2017+. GPU backends have separate compiler/driver prerequisites in the README.

```sh
git clone --recursive https://github.com/OpenImageDenoise/oidn.git
```


```sh
oidnDenoise -h
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/RenderKit/oidn/blob/master/README.md)

1. Install Git LFS before cloning; otherwise model/source files can be invalid even when Git reports success.
2. Use the released library/example or follow the exact backend-specific build guide; inspect oidnDenoise help for available options.
3. Prepare a noisy render and matching optional albedo/normal AOVs. Default CLI input is little-endian PFM; OpenEXR/PNG need an OpenImageIO-enabled rebuild.
4. Select the correct HDR/filter settings and inspect fine detail and sequences against a higher-sample reference before integrating the output.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/RenderKit/oidn/blob/master/README.md)

- **Hardware:** CPU requires x64 with SSE4.1 or ARM64. GPU is optional and backend-specific. No universal RAM/VRAM or total-disk minimum is established; render size and buffers determine memory use.
- **Software:** v2.5.1 documented. 64-bit OS and x64 SSE4.1 or ARM64 CPU. Optional GPU backend requirements differ for Intel SYCL, NVIDIA CUDA, AMD HIP and Apple Metal; training is a separate Linux/Python/PyTorch workflow and is not needed for bundled inference.
- **Platforms:** 64-bit Windows/Linux/macOS. Apple GPU requires macOS Ventura+. Documented GPU families include Intel Xe-series, NVIDIA Turing/Ampere/Ada/Hopper/Blackwell, AMD RDNA2–4 and Apple M1+. Check the exact current driver table rather than assuming every GPU works.

### License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [Source 1](https://github.com/RenderKit/oidn/blob/master/LICENSE.txt) [Source 2](https://github.com/RenderKit/oidn/blob/master/README.md)

- **Code:** Apache-2.0
- **Weights:** Complete Apache-2.0 software grant, including patent/NOTICE/redistribution conditions, was compared in full. Optional drivers, rendering hosts, third-party components and user-trained model/data rights remain separate. This review does not clear redistribution of every custom checkpoint.
- **Commercial:** Software use follows Apache-2.0. Complete Apache-2.0 software grant, including patent/NOTICE/redistribution conditions, was compared in full. Optional drivers, rendering hosts, third-party components and user-trained model/data rights remain separate. This review does not clear redistribution of every custom checkpoint.
- **Cost:** Local compute and any chosen renderer/driver stack. Apache code does not require an OIDN subscription; host licensing is separate.

### Why it merits attention

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. [Source 1](https://github.com/RenderKit/oidn/blob/master/README.md)

### Limitations

Denoising can lose detail or introduce artifacts; a fast preview is not ground truth. Auxiliary-buffer conventions, device memory, GPU buffer APIs and image-format support matter. A renderer must use its actual integrated version. Universal RAM/VRAM/disk minima were not established in this review. [Source 1](https://github.com/RenderKit/oidn/blob/master/README.md)
